Roles: President MARIA STEFANIA MONTECALVO Program, texts and objectives Professional status conferred by the degree.
Master's degree graduates will be able to work, with a specific degree of responsibility:
- in cultural centers, Italian and foreign, public and private, such as archives, libraries, superintendencies, foundations;
- study and research centers;
- publishing, communication and historical and literary dissemination industries;
- government and local institutions in the cultural services and recovery of activities, traditions and local identities sectors;
- journalistic offices.
Graduates may consider teaching in schools (in philological-literary, classical and modern, historical and historical-artistic disciplines) as a career, once the process of qualification for teaching has been completed and the competitions provided for by current regulations have been passed. Similarly, access to school management is through competition and after having gained a predetermined amount of experience.
Characteristics of the final exam.
Obtaining the Master's Degree in "Philology, Literatures and History" requires passing a final exam (master's degree exam), which consists of a public discussion, before a commission of teachers, of a paper that the student will have prepared under the guidance of a teacher from one of the scientific-disciplinary sectors provided for in the didactic regulations. The commission's evaluation is expressed in hundredths.
To be admitted to the degree exam, the student must:
- have passed the exams for obtaining all the credits required by the Study Course;
- have prepared a written paper that will be the subject of the degree exam.
Knowledge required for access.
Access to the Master's Degree Course in Philology, Literatures and History is open to students who hold a degree or university diploma of three years' duration, or another qualification obtained abroad, recognized as suitable.
Adequate basic preparation in linguistic, literary and artistic disciplines and related languages, and good written and oral communication skills are also required.
The curricular requirements necessary for admission to the Master's Degree Course in Philology, Literatures and History will be specified in the Didactic Regulations of the study course, as well as the methods of verifying personal preparation.
Learning skills.
Graduates must have developed the learning skills necessary for them to undertake further studies with a high degree of autonomy. Graduates in the Master's Degree courses of the LM 14 and LM 15 classes will acquire:
- adequate understanding of the interaction between literary and artistic forms in the varied landscape of the communication system of Western civilization;
- solid mastery of the methodological tools useful for professional updating and for continuous development of critical awareness of the general processes of communication and the more specific mechanisms of literary production and communication.
Graduates will be able to independently continue their studies aimed at:
- applying the methods and tools of learning useful for updating and deepening the contents studied;
- continuing studies in the various fields of philology with a high degree of autonomy and developing a flexible mindset that allows for rapid integration into working environments and easy adaptation to new situations;
- constant updating on the teaching tools available;
- autonomous learning throughout life;
- autonomous learning in Italian and English, as well as in another language possibly studied;
- critical analysis and conscious selection of primary and secondary documentation, as well as bibliographic material;
- assuming responsibility in the execution of projects, even complex ones, independently or in coordination with other subjects.
Learning methods: students will progressively acquire these skills in specialist courses and in the preparation of the degree thesis.
Verification methods: some topics discussed and evaluated during the exam will be explored in preparation for the drafting of the individual and final paper of the study program.
The courses of the master's degree program include teaching methodologies such as the analysis and resolution of different and complex problems, the integration of the various disciplines and group discussion; these methodologies promote the acquisition of skills related to learning and adaptation.
A useful tool for achieving these skills will be the degree thesis, which will allow the student to deal with new information, with laboratory activities and within a complex training context.
Communication skills.
Graduates must be able to communicate information, ideas, problems and solutions to specialist and non-specialist interlocutors. Graduates in the Master's Degree courses of the LM 14 and LM 15 classes will acquire:
- mature critical use of the main computer and telecommunications tools;
- solid mastery, in written and oral form, of at least one foreign language of the European Union, with particular attention to sector-specific languages;
- ability to communicate clearly and unambiguously with all operators in the sector;
- ability to use fluently, in written and oral form, at least one language of the European Union, in addition to Italian, with reference also to disciplinary lexicons;
- ability to identify and outline the essential elements of a process or situation, to develop an adequate model, and to verify its validity, so as to be able to make autonomous judgments that allow the graduate to relate to scholars in the field;
- ability to carry out advanced scientific research and collaborate with professionals in the same field;
- ability to communicate to specialists and non-specialists, in a clear and unambiguous manner, both in their native language and in the foreign language learned, the results of their studies;
- ability to conduct a scientific discussion using the methodologies and content learned;
- ability to use computer tools to present a scientific topic;
- ability to communicate results, methods and models subject to analysis and research to a specialized or general audience, in their own language and in at least one language of the European Union (primarily in English), both in written and oral form.
Master's degree graduates must be able to operate effectively as leaders of a project and a group that may be composed of people with expertise in different disciplines and at different levels. The master's degree graduate must also know:
- work and communicate effectively in larger contexts, both national and international;
- differentiate and adapt communication according to the audience;
- appropriately disseminate the results of scientific research protocols;
- manage and transfer information and develop communication and relational skills to relate to and integrate into the workplace;
- communicate both general concepts and specific technical content, as well as in Italian, also in another language of the European Union, with particular reference to English as it is primarily used in scientific disciplines.
Teaching tools: The didactic approach provides, in some characterizing courses and in the thesis work, the use of audio-visual equipment, as well as applications and tests that encourage active participation, a proactive attitude and the ability to communicate the results of the work carried out.
Learning methods: training activities that involve the final presentation of one's study topic.
Verification methods: evaluation of the expository skills during written and/or oral exams and during the presentation of the final thesis.

More information

-----

UNIVERSITY: University of Foggia
COURSE: [1244] BIOTECHNOLOGICAL, FOOD AND HUMAN NUTRITION SCIENCES
TYPE: Master's Degree
SECTION: INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS
URL: https://unifg.coursecatalogue.cineca.it/corsi/2024/10489

INFORMATION ON STUDY PLANS AND COURSES General information relating to the current academic year. Duration: 2 years. Degree program: 2020. Status: Open. Credits: 120 CFU. Language: Italian. Type of course: Master's Degree Courses. Regulations: D.M. 270/2004. Degree class: LM-9 - Class of Master's Degrees in Medical, Veterinary and Pharmaceutical Biotechnology. Interclass: LM-61 - Class of Master's Degrees in Human Nutrition Sciences. Inter-university course: No. Open access: Yes. Location: FOGGIA. Department: Department of Agricultural Sciences, Food, Natural Resources and Engineering. Website: https://www.unifg.it/it/studiare/corsi-di-laurea/lauree-magistrali/scienze-biotecnologiche-degli-alimenti-e-della-nutrizione-umana. Teaching method: Blended. Verification of knowledge required for access: No. Course teachers: View list. Admission requirements: Optional qualifications (to be chosen from the following): [TSS + L2], [Laurea di Primo Livello], [Titolo di Scuola Superiore], [TSS+LS], [Titolo di Scuola Superiore], [Laurea Specialistica], [TSS + L1], [Laurea], [Titolo di Scuola Superiore], [TS], [Titolo straniero], [TSS+LM], [Titolo di Scuola Superiore], [Laurea Magistrale]. Fees and contributions: Virtual stamp duty amount: €16 by 31/10/2024. Comprehensive contribution amount: €1990 by 31/01/2025. Recovery of A.DI.SU. fee: amount: €50 by 31/01/2025. Regional fee for the right to study: amount: €130 by 31/10/2024. Dates and deadlines: Application for abbreviated enrollment via web: 01/08/2024 - 30/11/2024. Deadline for application closure: 01/08/2024 - 30/09/2025. Deadline for application for course transfer: 01/08/2024 - 30/11/2024. Deadline for application for course transfer: 01/08/2024 - 30/11/2024. Deadline for application for suspension of studies: 01/08/2024 - 30/06/2025. Deadline for application for transfer out: 01/08/2024 - 30/11/2024. Enrollment via web: 01/08/2024 - 31/03/2025. Deadline for submission of self-certification: 01/08/2024 - 30/11/2024. Deadline for transfer: 01/08/2024 - 31/10/2024. Deadline for transfer: 01/08/2024 - 30/11/2024. Deadline for choice of curriculum via web: 01/08/2024 - 31/10/2024. Transfer in via web: 01/08/2024 - 31/12/2024. Roles: President: ROSARIA VISCECCHIA. Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree. Expert in food science and human nutrition. The main employment opportunities for the professional figure of "Expert in food science and human nutrition" are represented by freelance and entrepreneurial activities in the field of Life Sciences (2.3.1.1.1 - Biologists and related professions). Graduates will be able to register with the register of nutritionists after passing the state exam. The agro-food, distribution and catering industries, as well as the pharmaceutical industries and analytical and market research companies, represent potential employment opportunities in the private sector. These are complemented by employment opportunities in the public sector: healthcare facilities, regional and provincial bodies, universities and research centers. Finally, access to doctoral courses and specialist schools also provides an opportunity for research activities (2.6.2.2.1 - Researchers and graduate technicians in biological sciences).
Graduates who have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject areas may, as provided by current legislation, take part in competitions for secondary school teaching in the following teaching areas: Code A-15 - Health disciplines, Code A-28 - Mathematics and Science, Code A-31 - Food Science, Code A-50 - Natural Sciences, chemistry and geography, microbiology. Access to international organizations may be regulated by specific procedures.
Medical and pharmaceutical biotechnologist: The main employment opportunities for the professional profile "Medical and pharmaceutical biotechnologist" are represented by activities in research and public or private service laboratories, in the national health system and in similar structures of the European Union, in industrial sectors aimed at the production of biosanitary, pharmaceutical and diagnostic products, commercial or information activities in the field of the pharmaceutical and diagnostic industry, in structures operating in biotechnological diagnostics (analysis laboratories), in structures that carry out activities complementary to biotechnology, such as technology transfer, scientific publishing, forensic laboratories.
The medical biotechnologist can take part in competitions in the Special Investigative Departments of the Carabinieri and the State Police, which provide for this professional figure, and in competitions for the teaching class: Code A-50 - Natural Sciences, chemistry and geography, microbiology.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam (master's degree examination) consists of a public discussion, before a commission of teachers, of a written paper, prepared by the student based on original research of an experimental nature carried out at Universities, Research Centers, Hospitals, Local Health Units or other affiliated structures. The final exam aims to complete the educational path undertaken by the student, allowing him to perfect his skills in terms of knowledge, ability to apply knowledge, development of relational skills, communication skills and autonomy of judgment in the field of biotechnology, food science and human nutrition.
Knowledge required for access: Admission to the Master's Degree course in Biotechnology, Food Science and Human Nutrition is subject to the possession of a degree (or a three-year university diploma) or other equivalent qualification obtained abroad.
Pursuant to Article 6, paragraph 2, of D.M. 270/04, specific curricular requirements and the possession of adequate individual preparation are required for access to the course of study.
With regard to curricular requirements, these are considered to be met if the candidate holds a degree in Medicine and Surgery (LM-41 class), in the fields of Food Science and Technology (L-26), Agricultural Science and Technology (L-25), Biotechnology (L-2), Biological Sciences (L-13), Chemical Sciences and Technologies (L-27), Pharmaceutical Sciences and Technologies (L-29), Motor and Sports Sciences (L-22), in the fields of Health Technical Professions (L/SNT3), as well as in the equivalent degree classes of previous regulations or in relation to equivalent qualifications obtained abroad.
All candidates, regardless of the degree already obtained, are also required to have adequate skills in the use of the English language, at least at level B1, which can be verified as indicated in the Didactic Regulations.
The individual preparation of all candidates will be assessed through an entrance exam carried out according to the methods defined in the Didactic Regulations.
Learning ability: Master's graduates in Biotechnology, Food Science and Human Nutrition will acquire, within the educational path, an autonomous study method essential both for dealing with the continuous updating of knowledge and skills necessary for the profession, and for accessing the third level of university education in specific areas of food and nutrition.
The acquisition of these skills and their monitoring will take place in particular during the preparation of the paper relating to the final exam. The results of the ongoing tests and the profit exams also contribute to the verification of the same.
By involving students in seminar activities during the lessons, so that they are stimulated to present topics that they have prepared independently, we aim to teach them how to communicate clearly, results, ideas, conclusions and solutions to problems on biotechnological topics, being able to adapt the language to the interlocutor, specialist or non-specialist. The laboratory internship phase will also put students in a position to communicate information and effectively give instructions to technical staff with whom they will be in contact, providing all the necessary information to direct their work productively. The effectiveness of communication skills will be assessed during the profit exams and in particular during the drafting of the thesis and its presentation. On these occasions, the student must demonstrate that he is able to write a scientific report with competence and professionalism and to present it appropriately. Communication skills will include knowledge of the English language in a way that allows him to confidently present his results in scientific settings such as international conferences.
The master's graduate will develop the ability to evaluate and solve complex problems and to develop procedures for solving the cases presented in professional activity.
The master's graduate will be made aware of the social and ethical implications related to his work.
The master's graduate must be able to communicate clearly and with appropriate language information, ideas and solutions to problems related to food, nutrition and biotechnological applications to specialist and non-specialist audiences, in a national and international context, through correct written and oral forms. They must also be able to interact with a large and diverse audience to counter incorrect food trends that are often the cause of disease and incorrect cultural attitudes towards biotechnology, increasing instead the transparency of information and, therefore, understanding.
In the educational path, the master's graduate will acquire an autonomous study method essential both for dealing with the continuous updating of knowledge and skills necessary for the profession, and for accessing the third level of university education.
The use of interactive lessons and seminar activities in which students will play an active role will stimulate students to learn concepts independently from primary literature and allow students to test their learning skills by teaching them to use primary literature and databases naturally and competently, being able to assess their reliability. Specific assessments during the course and profit exams will be used to verify that the student has developed the learning skills that allow him to continue his training in a direct and autonomous way. Teaching tools: lectures, seminars and practical activities. Verification methods: exams, evaluation of written and/or oral reports and discussion of research projects by the student.
Communication skills: Master's graduates in Biotechnology, Food Science and Human Nutrition must be able to communicate clearly and with appropriate language information, ideas and solutions to problems related to the use of biotechnology, food and human nutrition to specialist and non-specialist audiences, in a national and international context, through correct written and oral forms. They must also be able to interact with a large and diverse audience of citizens to counter incorrect information in the field of biotechnology and incorrect food choices, increasing the clarity and correctness of information, the understanding of the problems and the related solutions. They must be able to clearly communicate the directives issued in the National and European context, the information and the correct biotechnological and food protocols. They have, in fact, acquired the communication and relational skills to be able to work in a group, know how to manage or coordinate other people within decision-making and negotiation processes. The acquisition of these skills will be favored through the carrying out of specific seminars and supported with the preparation of specific reports during the carrying out of the most professionalizing teachings. The monitoring of the achievement of learning outcomes in terms of communication skills will take place through individual and/or group seminars entrusted to the students and the presentation of essays on specific topics as well as during the profit exams of the individual teachings and, in particular, the final exam.
By involving students in seminar activities during the lessons, so that they are stimulated to present topics that they have prepared independently, we aim to teach them how to communicate clearly, results, ideas, conclusions and solutions to problems on biotechnological topics, being able to adapt the language to the interlocutor, specialist or non-specialist. The laboratory internship phase will also put students in a position to communicate information and effectively give instructions to technical staff with whom they will be in contact, providing all the necessary information to direct their work productively. The effectiveness of communication skills will be assessed during the profit exams and in particular during the drafting of the thesis and its presentation. On these occasions, the student must demonstrate that he is able to write a scientific report with competence and professionalism and to present it appropriately. Communication skills will include knowledge of the English language in a way that allows him to confidently present his results in scientific settings such as international conferences.

Roles: Course Coordinator ELISABETTA D'APOLITO, President ELISABETTA D'APOLITO
Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Manager, risk manager and management controller in banks and other credit institutions
- manager, risk manager and management controller in commercial and investment banks, in consumer credit companies and in leasing and factoring companies.
Expert and responsible for the various areas of intermediation within securities intermediaries, insurance companies and other financial investment companies
- portfolio manager at SIM, SGR, fiduciary companies, SICAV, insurance companies and other intermediaries;
- commercial manager in securities intermediaries, insurance companies and other financial investment companies.
Expert in the financial management of production and service companies, public and private, oriented towards innovation and internationalization
- financial manager in public and private companies;
- external consultant in innovation and internationalization processes;
- manager in investment banks and private equity and venture capital companies.
Specialist in economic systems
- economic analyst at public, national and supranational bodies;
- economic analyst within national and supranational institutions, agencies and research centers;
- analyst at rating agencies.
Analyst of the banking and financial system
- analyst at national and supranational bodies for the regulation and monitoring of the financial system;
- official at national and supranational bodies for the supervision of the banking system.
Financial consultant and chartered accountant
- Independent financial consultant;
- Chartered accountant (after passing the state exam for the authorization to practice the profession).
Characteristics of the final exam:
The final exam for obtaining the master's degree consists of an examination on the discussion before a Commission of a written dissertation, prepared under the guidance of a supervisor, relating to a subject specific to the master's study path. The dissertation must demonstrate critical skills and the ability to address, with original results and with good experimental documentation, the themes of the degree class. In the preparation of the thesis, the student must use recent relevant scientific literature and the analytical techniques specific to the training path followed. The theses will be analyzed through the anti-plagiarism software Compilatio.
Link to the Regulation of the final exam: https://www.economia.unifg.it/sites/sd01/files/allegati/08-11-2016/regolamento_laurea_triennale_e_magistrale.pdf
Knowledge required for access:
Pursuant to Article 6, paragraph 2, of D.M. 270/04, as this is a non-programmed study course, enrollment is subject to the possession of specific curricular requirements as well as the verification of adequate personal preparation.
With reference to the curricular requirements, these are considered to be met if the student holds a three-year degree, in classes 17 or 28 (ex D.M. 509/1999) or L-18 or L-33 (ex D.M. 270/2004), or a four-year degree in Economics and Commerce (before D.M. 509/1999), obtained at the University of Foggia or at other universities, or other equivalent qualifications obtained abroad.
If the three-year degree was obtained in other classes, the curricular requirements are considered to be met if the candidate demonstrates, with appropriate certification, that they have acquired in their university curriculum the minimum number of university training credits (CFU) indicated in the Didactic Regulations of the study course.
Any missing CFUs can be obtained through individual courses, also taken at other universities.
Admission to the Master's degree course in "Banking, Finance and Markets" is subject to the verification of the possession of adequate personal preparation, to be carried out through an interview to ascertain the knowledge and skills upon entry, which will take place at the times and according to the methods published on the website of the Department of Economics. Among the requirements verified during the interview, the possession of adequate basic preparation in the English language is included, which is the most widespread in economic and financial publications and debates. In particular, the 4 CFUs provided for the specialist knowledge of the foreign language will be automatically awarded to all those who possess a language certification of at least level B2, while for the others it will be necessary to attend the course and pass the exam to verify learning. Adequate basic computer skills are also required, to be ascertained by submitting standard certifications recognized at European level or by certifications that attest to the acquisition of adequate computer skills in their university curriculum.
Although the initial test to verify the basic knowledge possessed is mandatory, its failure does not preclude the possibility of enrollment.
Learning ability:
The Master's graduate must:
- be able to use the analytical tools and learning methods developed to deepen and update their knowledge independently;
- be able to identify the tools and training paths suitable for the development of their specialist knowledge and professional skills;
- be able to independently conduct studies and research on the topics covered in the study path.
Learning skills are acquired in the study path as a whole, with particular regard to the individual study provided, the preparation of individual projects, and the activities carried out for the preparation of the degree thesis. Learning skills are assessed through continuous verification methods during the training activities, requiring the presentation of data obtained independently, through the evaluation of the self-learning skills acquired during the preparation of the degree thesis.
Communication skills:
The Master's graduate must:
- have a good knowledge of a foreign language, with particular reference to the mastery of specialist language in economic subjects;
- possess adequate communication skills, be able to express themselves in writing and orally effectively and with a language appropriate for different interlocutors;
- be able to present and discuss business cases;
- be able to propose investment and financing solutions;
- be able to communicate with an investor or a company, in order to understand their financial needs.
Communication skills are developed in particular during the training activities that involve the preparation of written reports and documents and the oral presentation of the same, as well as through participation in group work and seminars. The acquisition of the aforementioned communication skills also takes place through the activity carried out by the student for the preparation of the final exam and the discussion of the same, and during the performance of the curricular internship.

Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Legal expert in security and personal data protection, corporate and labor.
- Official within Ministries and, more generally, within the Public Administration with tasks of managing data security (legal department, privacy, ICT, etc.).
- Legal consultant within corporate security. Corporate Security Manager. Data Protection Officer (more commonly known as DPO).
- Legal expert in impact assessment and data management.
- Official within the Authorities, with tasks of regulation in the sectors of communication and privacy. Legal consultant in the field of relations between Authorities, companies and public and private bodies.
- Legal expert in the processing of data relating to workplace accidents and occupational diseases and the health data of workers.
- Manager and member of the Prevention and Protection Service. Legal expert in analysis and design of workplace safety and environmental certification.
- Legal expert in human resources management, with reference to new types of contracts (remote work, telework, flexible work, platform work and crowdworking) and related emerging risks.
- Official in government security agencies.
- The degree obtained will allow, like other degrees, to compete for advancement in the ranks of the Police Forces.
Characteristics of the final exam:
The final exam (master's degree exam) consists of a public discussion, before a commission of teachers, of a written paper, prepared by the student based on original research of an experimental nature carried out at the University and at the bodies that hosted him during the internship or during the laboratory activities. The final exam, in fact, has the task of completing his training process, also of a practical type, allowing him to perfect his skills in terms of knowledge and to complete the acquisition of the ability to apply the knowledge already acquired within the internships or during the laboratory activities. The final exam will also allow the development of relational skills, communication skills and autonomy of judgment in the areas related to the legal, economic and technical aspects that relate to the field of security.
Knowledge required for access:
Admission to the Master's Degree Course in Legal Sciences of Security is subject to the possession of a degree (or a three-year university diploma) or other equivalent qualification obtained abroad.
Pursuant to Article 6, paragraph 2, of D.M. 270/04, specific curricular requirements are required for access to the course of study, including the possession of a linguistic certification relating to knowledge of the English language at a level not lower than B2 (or passing an equivalent level exam), as well as the possession of adequate personal preparation.
With regard to the curricular requirements, these are considered to be met if you have a degree in Legal Sciences (class L-14).
If the degree was obtained in other classes, the curricular requirements will be ascertained, according to methods defined in the Course Study Regulations and on the basis of the documentation produced by the candidate, by a commission, appropriately appointed by the Department Council, which will identify paths within the Master's Degree depending on the curricular requirements met and/or the result of the verification of personal preparation. These paths will lead to the achievement of the Master's Degree with 120 CFU, without additional training activities.
In addition to the possession of the curricular requirements, the adequacy of the personal preparation is also verified according to methods defined in the Course Study Regulations.
In particular, a commission will be responsible for ascertaining the individual preparation of all candidates, whatever the degree already obtained, through a test to verify the initial preparation.
The tests to verify the initial preparation will be carried out at least twice a year.
Learning ability:
The graduate will be able to easily and profitably undertake subsequent study and specialization paths, as well as engage in activities to update their professional training, in order to be adequately prepared for entry into a constantly evolving world of work.
The entrance exam provides an initial assessment of learning ability.
The appropriate weight given to individual study and the various methods of verifying the knowledge imparted in the training course allow the student to refine their cognitive tools over time.
The preparation of the final paper, which requires the student to understand new concepts independently, represents the main tool for verifying the acquisition of learning skills.
Communication skills:
Graduates must:
- acquire a correct legal language and a specialist terminology - also in English - in order to develop communication skills, also through the discussion in class of concrete cases and active participation in individual and group exercises;
- be able to communicate in writing and orally information, ideas, problems and solutions to specialist and non-specialist interlocutors, both national and foreign, in a clear and convincing manner;
- have the ability to listen and dialogue, negotiate, establish trusting relationships, communicate, work in a team. In particular, have the ability to design, implement, manage independently and have managed the processes dedicated to security and personal data protection, as well as risk management, both in the public and private sectors and to the threat to people and the territory.
The didactic approach provides for project activities where students can refine their communication skills by simulating the creation and management of the legal issues related to security and personal data protection processes, as well as prevention and safety in the workplace, and safety of people and the territory, both individually and, above all, in teams.
The acquisition of communication skills is verified through the active participation of the student in the classroom or in videoconference during the presentation of the results of individual or group work on topics or cases proposed by the teacher.
The verification of the acquisition of communication skills also takes place during the final exam.

Program, texts and objectives: Professional status conferred by the degree.
Biologist and related professions. The Master's graduate carries out his/her professional activity in:
- Public and private laboratories for chemical-clinical, biological, microbiological, genetic analysis. According to Presidential Decree 328/01, graduates can take the state exam for the qualification to practice the profession of biologist and subsequently obtain registration in the National Order of Biologists (section A). The Master's degree does not allow direct access to management roles, for which, in addition to registration in the National Order of Biologists, a further level of post-graduate training (Specialization School) and, in public structures, passing specific competitions is necessary;
- Clinical research centers;
- Universities and public and private research institutions;
- Scientific Investigation Department (RIS). This is not a direct employment opportunity, but it requires passing the competitions provided for by current regulations;
- Pharmaceutical, biotechnological, diagnostic companies, companies supporting basic and translational scientific research;
- Toxicology laboratories;
- Companies and organizations for quality certification;
- Communication, dissemination and scientific information agencies, specialized publishing houses (biomedical scientific publishing), web publications;
- Freelance activity in biomedical consulting;
- Secondary schools of the first and second cycle. Graduates who have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject groups may, as provided by current legislation, participate in the entrance tests for training courses for secondary school teachers.
Researchers and technicians with degrees in biological sciences. The Master's graduate in Clinical and Experimental Biology carries out his/her professional activity with responsibilities in:
- Universities and public and private research institutions;
- Companies with R&D in the biomedical field (e.g. pharmaceutical, biotechnological, diagnostic);
- Clinical research centers;
- Communication, dissemination and scientific information agencies, specialized publishing houses (biomedical scientific publishing) and web publications;
- Secondary schools of the first and second cycle. Graduates who have a sufficient number of credits in appropriate subject groups may, as provided by current legislation, participate in the entrance tests for training courses for secondary school teachers.
The Master's graduate who intends to carry out a professional role as coordinator of research activities needs to acquire higher levels of responsibility and autonomy through the path represented by the third level of training, the Doctoral Program, providing for a suitable period abroad.
Characteristics of the final exam: The final exam consists of the design, development, writing and discussion of a paper with original content, consistent with the educational objectives, resulting from a suitable experimental activity, on a specific topic under the guidance of a supervisor. The research activity related to the final exam, to be carried out in a university laboratory or in an external public or private body, national or international, allows the student to acquire skills in the scientific approaches of research in the biomedical field, mastery of the design and management of the scientific method of investigation, the experimental methodology and the tools and techniques for data analysis. Furthermore, the student develops transversal skills such as operational autonomy, critical thinking, updating and taking responsibility for projects and structures, as well as communication skills and the ability to apply knowledge and understanding. The acquisition of the educational credits relating to the thesis and the final exam (35 educational credits in total) is subject to the student's participation in an intense internship activity in a laboratory of the university or another public or private body. The duration of the thesis internship must be at least eight months. The written dissertation in English must have the typical form of a complete scientific work in terms of overall organization (Abstract, Introduction, Materials and Methods, Results, Discussion, Bibliography, Tables and Figures). The final exam concludes with the compilation and discussion of the thesis in English (oral presentation of a paper relating to the research activity carried out) before a special Commission which expresses the overall evaluation according to the methods defined in the Didactic Regulations of the Study Course.
Knowledge required for access: To be admitted to the Master's Degree course in Clinical and Experimental Biology, it is necessary to have:
- a degree in the L-13 (Biological Sciences) or L-2 (Biotechnology) classes as defined by D.M. 270/04, or in the equivalent classes as defined by D.M. 509/99;
or
- another first-level qualification obtained in Italy or abroad, recognized as suitable according to current regulations. In this case, the curricular requirement for access to the course is to have acquired:
- at least 12 CFU in non-biological basic disciplines: mathematics, physics and computer science MAT/01-09, FIS/01-08 and INF/01;
- at least 12 CFU in non-biological basic disciplines: chemistry: CHIM/01-CHIM/12;
- at least 40 CFU in the following scientific-disciplinary sectors: BIO/01, BIO/02, BIO/04, BIO/05, BIO/09, BIO/10 BIO/11, BIO/13 BIO/14, BIO/16, BIO/17, BIO/18, BIO/19, MED/03, MED/04, MED/07, MED/42.
Students must also have acquired English language skills at level B2 in the previous academic career or have certified these skills through appropriate certification.
The verification of the initial preparation will be carried out through a test aimed at evaluating the adequacy of the knowledge and skills previously acquired. Further details on the method of carrying out the test will be specified in the didactic regulations.
The Master's Degree course may provide for international students a special session and the appointment of a commission to verify the adequacy of their personal preparation, subject to the deadlines set by the call for applications for the possible award of scholarships (the deadline for which is expected around May). International students who have obtained a positive evaluation in the verification referred to in the previous point are exempt from the subsequent test to verify the adequacy of their personal preparation.
Learning skills: The Master's graduate in Clinical and Experimental Biology acquires skills that promote the development and deepening of skills, with particular reference to the consultation of specialized databases and the use of advanced knowledge tools for the continuous updating of knowledge. In particular, the Master's graduate:
- possesses learning skills that are necessary to undertake future studies with a reasonable degree of autonomy;
- is able to independently learn the most recent trends in international scientific research, emerging experimental procedures, basic and applied, and the development of innovative technologies and their applications in the relevant fields, using his/her own knowledge and/or scientific sources;
- is able to critically evaluate the results of experimental activities and to develop experimental strategies and research projects relating to his/her field of action;
- uses the computer tools necessary for accessing and using scientific literature in English and genomic, molecular and structural databases;
These skills allow the graduate in this Master's degree to access further levels of training (master's degrees, doctoral programs, specialization schools), in Italy or abroad, and to develop and organize paths of self-learning for continuous professional training.
The acquisition of these learning and self-learning skills is ascertained through continuous forms of verification during the training activities, in particular through the passing of profit exams, tutoring activities in the development of projects, specific seminar activities and through the evaluation of the self-learning skills acquired during the activity related to the final exam.
Communication skills: The Master's graduate in Clinical and Experimental Biology will have acquired adequate skills and tools for communication and information management with reference to:
- relational and communication skills to present his/her knowledge clearly and unambiguously, scientific arguments (research projects and research results) orally or in writing, also in international contexts, both scientific and popular, adapting the level of communication to the audience to which it is addressed;
- communication and skills useful for effective integration into working groups, also multidisciplinary, in particular within the management of laboratory activities, offering his/her specific contribution clearly and precisely;
- fluent and effective use of the English language, in written and oral form, with reference also to disciplinary vocabulary.
These written and oral skills are acquired, developed and verified during the courses, during the theoretical/practical laboratory activities, the seminar activities also in English, the training internship and the thesis project path, in Italy or abroad, which involves participation in lab meetings for the discussion of experimental data and monthly written and/or through the use of multimedia tools and the presentation of scientific articles (Journal Club). The internship period also allows to acquire the ability to interact with collaborators and technical staff. The acquisition of the aforementioned communication skills is also verified through the drafting of the final exam (in Italian or English) and the discussion thereof, on the occasion of the final exam in which the student is required to have fully acquired the expository and communication skills as well as the adequate command of language.

Program, texts and objectives
Professional status conferred by the degree.
Pedagogue
For graduates in the LM-85 and LM-50 classes, job opportunities fall within the areas of professional expertise of the 7th level of the European Qualifications Framework, as professionals at a senior level.
Specifically, graduates in Educational Sciences and Educational Design work in the public, private, corporate, and non-profit sectors, in non-governmental organizations, in the field of cooperation and local, national, and international educational services, as well as self-employed professionals.
As provided for in Article 594 of Law No. 205/2017, Official Gazette No. 302 of 29/12/2017, job opportunities relate to the various educational and training contexts, both in the socio-educational and socio-assistance sectors (limited to educational aspects), for people of all ages, in the areas of parenthood and family; cultural; judicial; environmental; sports and motor skills; integration and international cooperation. In particular, they work in the field of schools, municipalities (social services, public administration, leisure, sports, and cultural services), health companies (prevention and rehabilitation services), universities, services of the Ministry of Justice, public and private companies, businesses, and non-profit organizations, through educational, training, re-educational, guidance, recreational, cultural, and play activities, as experts and specialists in the design, evaluation, organization, and coordination of training, education, and socialization activities that require specific skills in pedagogy and training.
In particular, among the possible job opportunities, the graduate may perform the role of:
- Pedagogue
- Coordinator and manager of educational and training services
- Training designer
- Specialist in the management of training and updating processes
- Specialist in the organization of work training and in the processes of training and updating at work
- Specialist in the processes of recognition, evaluation, and certification of skills
- Specialist in the processes of school-work alternation and in the organization of training for apprenticeships, internships, and traineeships
- Guidance and placement counselor
- Head teacher and pedagogical coordinator of nurseries and other services for children.
Graduates of LM85 and graduates of LM 50 (who have acquired the necessary credits in their study plan for access to teaching) may perform teaching functions in class A-18, once the regulatory process for access to secondary school teaching positions has been completed.
Coordinator and manager of educational and training services and services for children
For graduates in the LM-85 and LM-50 classes, job opportunities fall within the areas of professional expertise of the 7th level of the European Qualifications Framework, as professionals at a senior level.
Specifically, graduates in Educational Sciences and Educational Design work in the public, private, corporate, and non-profit sectors, in non-governmental organizations, in the field of cooperation and local, national, and international educational services, as well as self-employed professionals.
As provided for in Article 594 of Law No. 205/2017, Official Gazette No. 302 of 29/12/2017, job opportunities relate to the various educational and training contexts, both in the socio-educational and socio-assistance sectors (limited to educational aspects), for people of all ages, in the areas of parenthood and family; cultural; judicial; environmental; sports and motor skills; integration and international cooperation. In particular, they work in the field of schools, municipalities (social services, public administration, leisure, sports, and cultural services), health companies (prevention and rehabilitation services), universities, services of the Ministry of Justice, public and private companies, businesses, and non-profit organizations, through educational and training activities, as experts and specialists in the organization and coordination of training, education, and socialization activities that require specific skills in pedagogy and training.
Job opportunities will mainly relate to the performance of the role of:
- Coordinator and manager of educational and training services
- Specialist in the management of training and updating processes
- Specialist in the organization of work training and in the processes of training and updating at work
- Head teacher and pedagogical coordinator of nurseries and other services for children.
Graduates of LM85 and graduates of LM 50 (who have acquired the necessary credits in their study plan for access to teaching) may perform teaching functions in class A-18, once the regulatory process for access to secondary school teaching positions has been completed.
Training designer
For graduates in the LM-85 and LM-50 classes, job opportunities fall within the areas of professional expertise of the 7th level of the European Qualifications Framework, as professionals at a senior level.
Specifically, graduates in Educational Sciences and Educational Design work in the public, private, corporate, and non-profit sectors, in non-governmental organizations, in the field of cooperation and local, national, and international educational services, as well as self-employed professionals.
As provided for in Article 594 of Law No. 205/2017, Official Gazette No. 302 of 29/12/2017, job opportunities relate to the various educational and training contexts. In particular, they work in the field of schools, municipalities (social services, public administration, leisure, sports, and cultural services), health companies (prevention and rehabilitation services), universities, services of the Ministry of Justice, public and private companies, businesses, and non-profit organizations, through educational, training, and guidance activities, as experts and specialists in the design and evaluation of training, education, and socialization activities that require specific skills in pedagogy and training.
Job opportunities will mainly relate to the performance of the role of:
- Specialist in the preparation, in public and private bodies, of training plans and projects, also based on regional, national, and international calls for proposals relating to the education, training, and training sector
- Specialist in the management of training and updating processes for the professionals of the structure (public and private) in which they work, in synergy with the other professionals present and based on their training needs
- Specialist in the processes of recognition, evaluation, and validation of skills in those public and private structures that deal with guidance and assessment/validation of skills.
Graduates of LM85 and graduates of LM 50 (who have acquired the necessary credits in their study plan for access to teaching) may perform teaching functions in class A-18, once the regulatory process for access to secondary school teaching positions has been completed.
Characteristics of the final exam.
The Master's Degree in Educational Sciences and Educational Design is obtained by passing the final exam (degree examination), which consists of the discussion of a degree thesis, with a high theoretical and/or experimental content, prepared by the student under the supervision of a lecturer. To be admitted to the degree examination, the student must:
- have passed the exams and obtained all the credits required by the study course
- have prepared the written paper that will be the subject of the final exam.
The degree is awarded after a discussion of the paper before a specially appointed Commission consisting of lecturers from the University of Foggia. The final grade is awarded out of 110, with possible honors and mention. The characteristics of the final exam are regulated by a specific Departmental Regulation.
Knowledge required for access.
The following can access the Master's Degree Course in Educational Sciences and Educational Design:
- graduates in Education Sciences and Training (class 18 of DM 509/1999 and class L-19 of DM 270/2004) who have a certificate of at least B2 level in a language of the European Union (in addition to Italian) or, alternatively, who have taken at least one basic and one advanced exam in a language of the European Union (in addition to Italian) during their undergraduate studies. For graduates in Education Sciences and Training (class 18 of DM 509/1999 and class L-19 of DM 270/2004) who do not have at least one of the two previous requirements, language skills will be assessed during the admission interview.
- graduates of all three-year and four-year study courses, including foreign ones if recognized as suitable, regardless of the class to which they belong, provided that in their university curriculum (including Master's degrees, Specialization Schools, etc.) they have acquired, in addition to fluent knowledge of at least B2 level of a European language (in addition to Italian), at least 48 CFU distributed in the following scientific-disciplinary sectors: 15 CFU in pedagogical and methodological-didactic disciplines (in at least one of the SSD M-PED/01, M-PED/02, M-PED/03, M-PED/04); 12 CFU in philosophical, psychological, sociological, and anthropological disciplines (in at least one of the SSD M-FIL/03, M-FIL/06, M-PSI/01, M-PSI/04, SPS/01, SPS/07, SPS/08, M-DEA/01); 12 CFU in historical, geographical, economic, and legal disciplines (in at least one of the SSD M-STO/01, M-STO/02, M-STO/04, M-STO/05, M-STO/06, IUS/01, IUS/07, M-GGR/01, SECS-P/10) and 9 CFU relating to knowledge of a foreign language and IT skills. The requirement relating to foreign language skills will be considered satisfied if the student has taken at least one basic and one advanced exam in a language of the European Union during the previous study cycle or by submitting a certificate of at least B2 level; otherwise, language skills will be assessed during the admission interview.
In any case, solid initial preparation in the disciplines that characterize the Master's degree course is required for access to the course, which will be verified in accordance with Article 6, paragraph 1, of DM 270/04.
The minimum knowledge required in the various disciplines and the methods of assessment will be indicated in the Regulations of the Master's Degree.
Learning skills.
The Master's degree graduate must develop learning skills aimed at: functionalizing the acquired training to the design of subsequent training courses, demonstrating a high degree of critical autonomy; carrying out analysis, synthesis, and critical thinking to correlate their training with the profession to be exercised in socio-educational contexts and work environments, in relation to the specific needs of the territory; carrying out constant monitoring, in the form of self-assessment, of their knowledge and skills in order to be able to update/integrate/deepen the reference knowledge.
The learning outcomes expected in relation to learning skills are achieved through the preparation - during the lessons/exercises - of activities for in-depth autonomous and motivated study, through research (bibliographic, archival, etc.) also with the support of digital media, as well as through the preparation of textual and/or multimedia products, presented in plenary sessions for discussion with the other students in the course and with the lecturer.
Learning skills are verified through the production of written and oral reports as well as in the written and/or oral tests provided as final verification tests.
Communication skills.
The Master's degree graduate in Educational Sciences and Educational Design must possess communication skills to: ensure fluent mastery of a language of the European Union, in written and oral form, in addition to Italian, and demonstrate possession and use of disciplinary lexicons; promote the understanding of the cultural and applied contents that characterize the practices in educational and training contexts; manage and transfer information through facilitated communication of information in the coordination and supervision structures at the various levels of socio-educational services, adapting and differentiating the codes of communication according to the recipients and the different operational situations; communicate effectively in the various national and international contexts for dissemination and exchange of information relating to scientific research and educational practices; develop skills in the use of multimedia technologies and distance learning systems for communication purposes in the various areas of competence; promote communication and relationship skills in the activities of coordination, supervision, and management of socio-educational services and in the creation of networks between operators of educational and training services.
The learning outcomes expected in relation to communication skills are achieved through the preparation, during the lessons, of moments of shared discussion between peers and with the lecturer as well as through the use of multimedia supports during the exercises and group work activities. Oral communication will then be supplemented by written communication activities through the preparation of textual and/or multimedia products (essays, project work, power points, videos, etc.), presented in plenary sessions.
Communication skills are verified through shared discussion meetings between peers and with the lecturer as well as through the use of multimedia supports during the exercises as well as in the written and/or oral tests provided as final verification tests.
